## STORY ENGINE AUTONOMOUS BATCH ORCHESTRATOR — first full wave stopped / Editor headroom QA pending

Guy approved replacing manual A/B/C selection for future commissions with an

## STORY ENGINE VNEXT MUSICAL READ-ALOUD POLISH — technical and artifact PASS / external editorial PASS / Guy product acceptance recorded above

Guy accepted the externally editorially passed Dini cake story as a substantial narrative improvement and asked for one further oral-reading pass: selective rhyme, sound-play and cadence where they strengthen comedy and motion, without converting the story into a rhyming poem. The accepted story remains immutable; this milestone adds a general, staging-only polish route rather than editing that candidate in place.

- `STORY_MUSICAL_READ_ALOUD_POLISH_CHARTER.md` permits natural internal/end rhyme, onomatopoeia, sound-play, rhythmic repetition and page-ending cadence, but explicitly rejects rhyme quotas, filler, inverted syntax, forced synonyms, slogans, sing-song repetition and any weakening of Hebrew clarity or causality.
- Polish locks title/metadata, 12-page order, events, causal mechanism, child agency, Dini's behavior, locations, important objects, climax, payoff and ending. Complete gender chips remain mandatory, and a rhyme may not depend on only one gender branch.
- A new `materialize-musical-polish-pilot` command accepts only a canonical draft bound to a closed editorial `pass` with zero issues and zero revision priorities. It creates a new content-addressed staging prompt and manifest; `revise`/`reject`, malformed drafts, unsafe paths and output-root reuse fail closed.
- Editor authority v3 makes musicality an optional amplifier, never a quota. It diagnoses rhyme only when it forces language, introduces filler/slogans, breaks personalization, becomes sing-song or weakens causality. The prior Editor authority remains historical; a returned polish cannot replace the accepted candidate without a fresh Editor v3 `pass` and Guy's product acceptance.
- The zero-cost Dini commission is `outputs/story-engine-vnext-dini-cake-musical-polish-20260814-v1/dragon_dini_adventure_wobble_cake_convoy_brief_v1.musical-polish.01932bcf5087d27c004cd63d53c56196afdb3b5b9e5a28724239d5be2cfd3b16.md` (9,498 bytes; raw SHA-256 equals the filename digest). It binds exact accepted draft `1e401854…`, exact Editor PASS `bed8053e…` and charter digest `28335f94…`.

Tests prove pass-only routing, exact-draft embedding, content addressing, output-root reuse refusal, canonical intake, gender-chip rejection, and the absence of Architect, full Editor and image-direction authority from the Writer prompt.

TypeScript and the full **19-file resource-intensive phase passed** with clean diagnostics; the changed 12-test materializer suite also passed under ordinary full-suite load. ## STORY ENGINE VNEXT FUNCTIONAL-QUALITY PILOT — independent technical PASS / product acceptance pending

Guy selected Architect option A and judged its resulting Dini cake draft a material improvement, while identifying that the middle still repeats one dramatic function, the adventure lacks one memorable comic peak and the child mostly observes until late. - The scope remains the single Dini cake pilot. The other 17 prepared slots, approved story banks, v2 18-slot materializer, Wizard/runtime and Production are unchanged.
- Story Architect v2 now requires an immediate visible disruption, causal room for one large visual-comedy escalation, meaningful child-initiated action before the climax, differentiated dramatic problems and one significant idea not predictable from the nucleus. These are functional acceptance boundaries, not prescribed events.
- `Child discovery` is replaced by the broader `Child agency arc`. The Writer receives exactly four compact drafting priorities and retains freedom over dialogue, transitions, local action, humor, rhythm, page allocation and the concrete solution.
- Post-draft QA is a separate diagnostic-only Editor stage. ## R1D-WIZARD-SELLABILITY-CHECKOUT-UNBLOCK — independent technical PASS / push and QA deployment pending

Guy reported that a QA book-creation flow stopped before fake payment. Vercel runtime inspection for the reported evening showed Wizard traffic but no `/api/orders`, `/api/checkout`, or fake-payment request. Authenticated browser reproduction on the exact QA deployment `dpl_FgJuU2HpfavbAxUspiCbBr7M2Xeo` at pushed commit `388dc4336c17487b9c0ef35da2f98ec8000c7db7` reached the product step and found all three product cards disabled as “coming soon”. No order or payment action was performed.

- Root cause: `public/JS/wizard.js` used the internal `selectable` readiness field for customer product availability. That field intentionally becomes false when no QA candidate or Production render-qualified package is available, even while the matrix's independent `sellable` field is true. Internal render readiness therefore leaked into the earlier customer purchase gate and stopped the flow before order creation.
- The general correction makes both product-card rendering and restored-selection validation consume `sellable`. Missing or non-sellable directions remain fail-closed. QA authoring metadata, candidate identity, Production render qualification, `/api/orders` `enforceMvpOrderSlot`, fake-payment flags/authentication, pricing, checkout, generation and Reader behavior are unchanged.
- Direct executable regressions run the real `renderProductCards` function with the two deliberately contradictory states: `sellable:true/selectable:false` remains enabled, while `sellable:false/selectable:true` is disabled and cleared. The API contract separately proves that real Production can report customer sellability while QA/render `selectable` remains false.
- Focused validation passes **6 files / 65 tests**, deterministic TypeScript and `git diff --check`. This includes the complete 37-test fake-payment gating suite, MVP matrix, Wizard matrix API, render readiness, the new customer gating contract and the workload classifier.
- The one literal `npm run check` ran once and was not retried. TypeScript passed; all **19/19 resource-intensive files** passed with valid diagnostics and no timeout, RPC/IPC, reporter, launch, signal, teardown or protocol failure. Ordinary reported the six established missing ignored-output fixture failures, the already documented pre-existing stale measurement-runner string assertion in `r1d-dini-bar-five-page-measurement-authority.spec.ts`, and one stale workload count caused solely by this milestone's new test file. The mechanical count was corrected from `295/276` to `296/277`; the focused classifier, full focused set and TypeScript passed afterward. The literal full gate was not rerun. The pre-existing fixture/string failures remain a separate repository/release HOLD.
- Claude Code independently reviewed exact immutable range `388dc4336c17487b9c0ef35da2f98ec8000c7db7..d5bfdd241eda7be1716f392bf559951fe7cb1708` read-only and returned **PASS** with zero BLOCKER, zero MAJOR and zero MINOR. It independently reproduced the exact **6 files / 65 tests**, fake-payment **37/37**, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, one-commit/zero-merge topology, the seven-file pathset and package/lockfile identity. It confirmed that `wizard.js` is the only customer matrix consumer, missing/non-sellable directions fail closed, the shipped function is executed by the regression, and server order authority plus payment/pricing/QA/render paths remain unchanged. Claude did not rerun the literal full repository gate.
- Claude retained five advisory NOTE findings only: the pre-existing client fail-open display when the matrix never loads (with server order enforcement still fail-closed), a currently unreachable strictness asymmetry if `sellable` were absent, source-slice and source-string test brittleness, and a pre-existing positive order-test skip pattern. ## R1D-READER-FLEXIBLE-PAGE-CURL - prior geometry PASS / moving paper-frame follow-up locally PASS / independent re-gate and product acceptance pending

Guy identified that the desktop physical page turn still read as a rigid board. Repository inspection confirmed the cause: one 180-degree parent rotation plus one 14-degree outer hinge created only two moving planes. The correction is general across books and changes only the existing desktop physical-sheet renderer.

- The sheet is now a deterministic connected mesh of **12 vertical paper strips**. Every strip receives its own rotation, but its centre is integrated from the preceding strip's outer edge, preserving edge continuity throughout forward and backward turns.
- A bounded smooth S-profile produces multiple local angles during the turn, approaches zero curvature at the bound spine and outer sheet edge, and returns every strip to an exactly coplanar state at rest and landing. The book frame, 560ms budget, navigation state, incoming/outgoing content, mobile behavior and reduced-motion skip remain unchanged.
- Guy's screen recording exposed a separate endpoint defect: the animated source sheet and static destination page have unequal measured rectangles, while the old mesh landed as though both pages had the same width and position. The component also completed the React transition in the same animation frame that wrote the final pose, so the overlay could disappear before that exact endpoint painted; its cast shadow remained at full opacity until removal.
- The endpoint correction measures both source and destination rectangles once, continuously morphs horizontal position, width, vertical offset and height into the real destination geometry, fades the cast shadow to zero at both endpoints, and waits one additional animation frame before replacing the overlay with the static spread. The solution is derived from Reader layout geometry and is independent of book, story, child or page content.
- Guy's follow-up recording exposed a remaining geometric knee at the physical pivot. At half turn, the old cosine-heavy profile already displaced the spine-nearest strip by approximately **28.2 degrees** from the hinge tangent. The corrected S-profile concentrates curvature in the paper body while keeping both sheet boundaries within **2 degrees** of the hinge tangent at half turn; the interior still exceeds **25 degrees** of local bend. This is a geometry correction rather than a mask or timing workaround.
- Browser verification on the tracked eight-page Bunny/Bar QA book confirmed 12 live strips, both forward and backward completion, no Next error overlay and no console errors. The first visual pass exposed per-strip shadow banding; that was removed before handoff. Final curvature shading is deliberately subtle and no per-strip box shadow remains.
- Latest focused validation passes **6 files / 41 tests**, including direct connected-edge geometry, coherent spine/outer tangents with interior curl in both directions, unequal source/destination landing geometry, non-rigid mid-turn shape, exact flat endpoints, mirrored forward/backward three-stop shading, Reader navigation/narration/storytime, tracked-QA routing and open-book layout. Deterministic TypeScript and `git diff --check` pass. Slow-motion browser sampling against the real Reader confirmed a continuous pivot and distributed interior curl; live endpoint sampling remains within `0.8px` of the destination rectangle (the intentional strip overlap only), with zero endpoint shadow and zero console warning/error.
- Claude Code independently reviewed exact range `640476ddfaf831ede8eca6e62f7529eb0c4f2107..0c267bb8e224f06a2f97e8dec994576fca075d3e` and returned **technical PASS** with no BLOCKER or MAJOR and two non-blocking MINOR findings. MINOR-1 identified the stale backward two-stop shade override; MINOR-2 identified missing disclosure of the easing change and removed crease element. Both were corrected in the focused QA-fix range. Claude's N1-N5 remain advisory, and this PASS grants no Guy product acceptance.
- Claude Code independently micro re-gated exact correction range `0c267bb8e224f06a2f97e8dec994576fca075d3e..0d1490effb3fa2ca2b1f17298cccbcf715ba75af` and returned **PASS**, independently closing MINOR-1 and MINOR-2 with no BLOCKER or MAJOR. Its new non-blocking MINOR-3 was a stale `38`-test count in the durable records; the count is corrected here to the independently reproduced **39**. Its N1-N5 remain advisory, the stale `.next` CSS is an untracked rebuild artifact rather than source, and no product/visual acceptance follows from the technical PASS.

It independently reproduced **6 files / 41 tests**, TypeScript and `git diff --check`; measured both sheet boundaries at `1.263deg` from the hinge tangent, interior curl at `28.660deg`, worst adjacent-edge separation at `2.278e-13px`, exact endpoint landing, unchanged texture ordering and the production `560ms` duration. Perspective authority is now `6400px`; each strip receives the inverse vertical projection compensation `1 - z / perspective`, cancelling only perspective-driven height growth while leaving horizontal curl and depth intact. A page-local top/bottom plus physical-outer-edge layer bends with the sheet, and a narrow binding clamp derived from the measured gap between `OPEN_BOOK_PAGE_BOXES.leftPage` and `.rightPage` keeps the photographed gutter above the pivot. It contains no book, story, page or locale literal and leaves content, timing, navigation, reduced motion, mobile and Production unchanged.
- Focused validation for this top/bottom/spine correction passes **6 files / 44 tests**, deterministic TypeScript and `git diff --check`. New direct regressions prove the binding clamp covers the measured gap while staying under 3% of the spread, the vertical compensation exactly neutralizes the CSS projection factor in both directions, and the moving edge plus static clamp remain in the shared Reader contract. This is locally verified technical evidence only; QA deployment, independent Claude Code review and Guy visual acceptance remain pending.

## R1D-READER-PRODUCTION-EXPERIENCE-POLISH — independent technical PASS / product acceptance pending

Guy asked to continue in order while Claude Code works separately on the website: first connect the rendered QA book to the real Reader, preserve the physical page turn, remove the developer-viewer feel and prevent dense mobile story text from being clipped. The implementation is generic across stories, children and companions.

- `ReaderV2` now accepts one closed discriminated book source: an authenticated real order or a server-resolved tracked QA fixture. The existing order URL, access-key fetch, retry/regeneration and exit behavior remain the production path. QA never synthesizes an order, database row or access key.
- New QA-only `/dev/reader?dir=...` admits only repository-owned tracked fixtures behind the existing local/Preview guard and feeds their payload into the same Reader navigation, narration, storytime, end-state, mobile and physical-sheet controller used by a real book. `/dev/viewer` remains the library/debug surface and now offers `open in READER` only when a safe production-Reader target exists.
- Mobile page layout now derives a typed `overlay | paper_panel | captionless` presentation from text treatment plus bounded character/word/sentence density. Short copy can remain over art; dense copy receives a separate scroll-safe paper panel, so complete prose remains available instead of extending beyond the `100dvh` scene and being clipped. Mobile edge controls are 44×44px.
- Live browser verification on the tracked eight-page Bunny/Bar book exposed and corrected one real render-loop defect: the QA path had inherited a newly allocated default `devLayoutFlags` object on every render. A module-stable empty authority now prevents the effect from reloading the book indefinitely. After correction, the browser showed no maximum-depth error, the start control worked and a forward transition rendered the existing two-segment paper sheet while the outer book frame remained static.
- Focused validation passes **6 files / 36 tests**, deterministic TypeScript, `git diff --check` and a complete `npm run build`. The first build command stopped before Next compilation because the live QA server held Prisma's Windows engine DLL; after stopping only that local server, the replacement completed Prisma generation, compilation and all 36 static pages. It covers page direction/restart, physical-sheet source wiring, narration selection, storytime dwell, open-book layout, source isolation, tracked-fixture/real-order Reader routing, dense mobile presentation and the stable default authority.
- Literal `npm run check` ran once and was not retried. TypeScript passed. The ordinary phase reported the six established missing ignored-output fixtures plus one pre-existing stale measurement-runner string assertion; no Reader assertion failed. The resource phase later failed after four subprocess-oriented test failures/timeouts and three `onTaskUpdate` RPC timeouts, with a valid diagnostic protocol. These repository-wide baseline/infrastructure failures are recorded, not hidden or treated as Reader PASS. The milestone's focused Reader evidence remains green; the full repository/release gate remains HOLD.
- No provider, credential, narration generation, image generation, storage/database, payment, Production promotion/deployment or push occurred. The website redesign remains isolated in Claude Code's separate worktree and branch.

Claude Code independently reviewed immutable range `d328b3e4220101eb642f09270bc340fe494af477..773f364fddada45eee236a8e4876bb93ae673eef` read-only and returned **TECHNICAL PASS** with zero BLOCKER, zero MAJOR and zero MINOR. It reproduced the exact 6-file / 36-test focused set, TypeScript and `git diff --check`; confirmed the preserved order path, tracked-fixture isolation, Production exclusion, dense-text classifier, 44px mobile controls, unchanged page-turn/narration/dwell behavior, stable default authority and documentation fidelity. Six advisory notes retain the dead `'outputs'` argument, future inline-source identity risk, two untested classifier boundaries, non-reproducible browser evidence, the separate repository-gate HOLD and deliberate Preview reachability. These are advisory only and grant no product, visual, deployment or release acceptance.

## R1D-CHILD-EXPRESSION-AND-STYLE-FIDELITY - bounded LOW proof complete / product review pending

Guy's Dini/Bar five-page review exposed two direct visual defects: Bar repeated the supplied photo's open-mouth smile across incompatible story beats, and the smaller page-3/page-4 child placements simplified toward Dini's mascot language. The relevant proof states are `subdued` for crowded-out/quiet, `wary` for too-tight spatial pressure, and `restrained_amusement` for an almost-smile. Ambiguous pages receive attentive closed-mouth neutrality rather than the source photo's expression.
- Approved Blueprint camera, placement, cast, action, prop and layout authority are unchanged. When the child occupies a small approved region, a geometry-derived guard requires the same semi-naturalistic human identity inside that region and forbids chibi/mascot/companion anatomy without moving or enlarging the placement.

It can render an explicit bounded page subset, allowing pages 1/3/4 to be remeasured without buying replacements for retained pages 2/5.

- The bounded proof completed exactly three sequential `gpt-image-2` LOW calls for pages 1, 3 and 4 at `1024x1536`, with zero transport retries, no fallback, no Vision and no remote database/storage. - The proof resolves the primary defect: page 1 changes from a broad open smile to a subdued closed-mouth expression; page 3 becomes attentive/uncertain rather than gleeful; page 4 becomes surprised with a naturally parted mouth rather than another fixed grin. The only observed `404` was the unrelated local `favicon.ico`. The Reader currently navigates by page replacement; a true page-curl/turn animation remains a separate UI gap.

## R1D-VITEST-ON-TASK-UPDATE-RPC-STABILITY - independent implementation PASS; MINOR-1 corrected pending micro re-gate; repository gate HOLD at established fixture baseline

Guy approved nine architectural decisions for the separate Vitest worker-to-main RPC stability milestone. The implementation preserves Node `22.19.0`, Vitest `3.2.4`, Tinypool `1.1.1`, isolated forks, file parallelism, the ordinary four-worker ceiling, the fixed 60-second RPC timeout, the 5-second test timeout, the 10-second teardown timeout, all dependency versions, and the lockfile.

- Installed locked source proves the path: the runner batches task-result packs; the worker calls and awaits `rpc().onTaskUpdate`; fork transport uses V8 serialization and `process.send`; bundled birpc owns the fixed 60-second acknowledgement timer; the main process applies updates and awaits reporter callbacks before acknowledging. The exact cause remains uncertain among event-loop starvation, IPC backlog, reporter/output pressure, serialization volume, and correlated subprocess/filesystem load.
- A repository-owned taxonomy distinguishes `on_task_update_rpc_timeout`, `test_timeout`, `reporter_or_ipc_failure`, `launch_failure`, `signal_or_exit_failure`, `teardown_or_termination_failure`, and `diagnostic_protocol_failure`. The opt-in reporter writes only bounded closed records to inherited fd 3. Vitest stdout/stderr remain directly inherited; no raw test payload, prompt, credential, error body, or arbitrary output body is captured or persisted.
- The canonical inventory is derived from the same include policy consumed by `vitest.config.ts`. Missing manifest targets, noncanonical paths, duplicates, case aliases, overlap, phase extras, inventory omissions, unsupported evidence, and malformed policy fail before Vitest starts.
- The evidence-backed resource manifest contains 19 unique specs: seven previously measured whole-repository scanners plus thirteen direct child-process specs, with one overlapping legacy-boundary spec. New canonical specs default to the computed ordinary complement.
- `npm run check` now retains its operator contract while invoking a repository-local Node supervisor. `npm test` remains `vitest run`; `npm run test:watch` remains `vitest`. The supervisor launches ordinary once with isolated forks, file parallelism, and at most four workers, then resource-intensive once with isolated forks, file parallelism, and at most two workers. It uses the installed `node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs`, `shell:false`, no eval, no npx, no network, no fallback, no retry, and aggregates both exits without short-circuiting.

## R1D-PVB-D1A1B1-DRAFT-AUTHORITY-AND-REFERENCE-DOMAIN-HARDENING - independent technical PASS / QA closeout

The approved nine-decision architecture is implemented as a general, story-neutral compiler and authority cutover. Claude Code independently reviewed exact range `07adf0997eb7330c026fb5c395cf77e359e89e4f..5a51fea3da01eb9a4ad8cac5534b80809bd939de` and returned technical **PASS** with zero BLOCKER, zero MAJOR, and two non-blocking MINORs. Codex records Claude's verdict; it does not self-award technical PASS.

- Compiler-owned action `checkId` values derive from exact same-page `beatId` bindings. The model no longer authors them, coverage is rewritten atomically, array order does not affect identity, and missing/duplicate/conflicting bindings fail as terminal reference-domain errors without a semantic repair.
- Page zones now expose explicit spatial nodes and typed relations. Set Board areas carry exact one-to-one or declared one-to-many zone projections. The compiler projects area geometry only through those bindings and rejects absent, cross-location, duplicate, or ambiguous mappings.
- Fixed architecture remains spatial geometry. Stable fixed objects are compiler-derived only from exact recurring-prop bindings; draft-authored `fixedObjects` are forbidden. Unary `centered_in` cannot carry `objectId`, while binary relations require it.
- Deterministic normalization happens before semantic validation and repair selection. The story-neutral regression matrix proves the captured `37/5/6/1` classes with no story literals in production code, no provider repair, and terminal ambiguity handling.
- Current authority is VC schema `vc-schema/v3`, draft `vc-draft-schema/v11`, prompt/repair family v7, Action Semantic Coverage v4, Set Board/registry/content-policy v4/v4/v3, authoring request/receipt/readiness/candidate v8/v7/v5/v5, B0 materialization/manifest/verifier v4/v6/v6, pre-live readiness evidence v5, Execution Request/readiness/result v5/v5/v3, and execution materialization input/result v3/v3. Prior lifecycle artifacts remain classified as immutable historical evidence and cannot become current authority.

## R1D-PVB-D1A1B1-POST-PREFLIGHT-EXECUTION-HARNESS-HARDENING — local implementation awaiting independent QA

Guy approved a repository-owned, story-neutral replacement for the hand-authored post-preflight PowerShell harness that failed at parse time during the exhausted diagnostic attempt. The implementation is additive: the existing authoring CLI, canonical B0 verifier, B0 schemas/artifacts, request/prompt/model/timeout/call and repair budgets, retry/fallback policy, and manifest `futureLiveCommand` remain unchanged.

### Implemented boundary

- `canonical-live-execution-request/v1` binds structured Git/topology authority, canonical B0 manifest linkage, exact preservation fences, expected-absence checks, credential isolation, and the exact future-command identity. `canonical-live-execution-readiness/v1` emits bounded sanitized reason codes and evidence.
- Public Node modes are strictly `verify` and future `live`. The launcher accepts no arbitrary command, shell text, eval, inline PowerShell, story-specific literal, or caller-defined child environment.
- The readiness core composes `verifyCanonicalLiveRequestBundle` as the sole B0 authority. It does not reimplement payload-domain digest, Story Source, policy, model, budget, or future-command validation.
- Git inspection uses fixed exact argv with `shell:false`: expected top-level, symbolic branch, HEAD, tracked and untracked cleanliness, explicit refs, and explicit divergence. Preservation fences accept only contained canonical file paths and verify byte length plus SHA-256 while rejecting traversal, globs, aliases, symlink/junction escape, and hard links.
- `verify` is read-only and cannot inspect a real credential source or cross provider, network, storage, database, or write boundaries. Future `live` reruns the same readiness core immediately before credential access, rejects ambient key authority, reads only `OPENAI_API_KEY` from the declared source after all gates, constructs a minimal platform-aware environment, and spawns only the exact manifest command through `process.execPath`, exact argv, `shell:false`, and `windowsHide:true`. It has no retry or fallback and propagates child error, signal, nonstandard exit, and bounded-output failure.
- A fixed repository-owned zero-cost probe exercises the same process-runner mechanics without accepting a command, credential, prompt, story literal, or external boundary. Unlike the real future-live child, the probe always runs the fixed local synthetic child and can never invoke the manifest command or provider/write behavior.
- Canonical import preflight remains separate and was not executed. Any future separately authorized sequence remains `verify -> one preflight -> verify -> live`, with `live` internally verifying again before credential access.

## R1D-PVB-D1A1B1-PROVIDER-CALL-FAILURE-DIAGNOSTICS — local implementation awaiting independent QA

Guy approved the Decision Gate and all nine binding architectural decisions. The implementation starts from exact Attempt-6 commit `f69bc6c63974c5f8c0bf04ad397baa025041b9d1`; code and tests are committed at `4d006e8bfafe8bb8541954977370cef69c1ca97c`.

### Implemented boundary

- One story-neutral module, `providerFailureDiagnostics.ts`, owns bounded phase/failure enums, structured SDK/status/code classification, sanitized observations, and canonical `provider-call-failure-evidence/v1` construction. It has no OpenAI SDK import, circular dependency, story ID, child, companion, beat, action, or page-specific policy.
- The OpenAI Responses adapter injects the installed SDK error classes into the neutral classifier. Its guarded fetch records local dispatch immediately before delegated fetch, records an actual `Response` and status afterward, digests only the bounded `x-request-id`, preserves `maxRetries: 0`, and changes no destination, method, model, timeout, request body, schema, prompt, call budget, repair budget, retry, or fallback policy.
- The additive failure sidecar links the exact request, source snapshot, receipt, and failed attempt. It persists only bounded enums, approved provider/endpoint/model labels, canonical body/options digests, an optional request-ID digest, `billingState: unknown_no_usage`, and explicit non-authorization boundaries. It cannot contain an API key or key hash, raw request ID, error/message/cause/stack, headers, prompt, body, response, arbitrary provider code, or arbitrary parameter value.
- Legacy receipt `providerReached` behavior is unchanged and remains adapter-invocation compatibility evidence only. The sidecar separately records `adapterInvoked`, `credentialReadSucceeded`, `transportDispatchStarted`, and `httpResponseReceived`; local dispatch is not represented as provider receipt.
- Failure persistence order is receipt, provider-failure sidecar, readiness. A sidecar write failure stops before readiness/candidate. Successful authoring creates no sidecar. A plain failure from an unknown injected adapter becomes `unclassified_adapter_failure` with no credential, dispatch, HTTP, status, or request-ID claim.
- Request v4, receipt v4, readiness v2, candidate v2, B0 artifacts, payload-domain digests, pricing, usage accounting, and all production authorization semantics remain unchanged.

Guy approved the general zero-cost root-cause milestone from immutable Attempt-5 base `853d700f14a7263b824d7b4146a54a5e36680abd`. The implementation is committed locally at `91f823b7c782880bbf4b1716fb5da7e40c71b539`.

## R1D-PVB-D1A1B1A — Provider Usage Evidence Hardening

Guy approved this zero-cost corrective milestone and all nine governing decisions after the D1A1B1 live attempt exposed incomplete canonical GPT-5.6 usage evidence. The approved point-in-time authority is that Responses usage contains disjoint cached-read and cache-write input buckets, cache writes are billed at `$6.25/M`, and the existing conservative reservation remains safe. This task performed no new pricing or documentation lookup.

### Implemented behavior

- Canonical provider evidence is now `openai-responses-authoring-evidence/v2`; newly produced durable authoring receipts are `visual-contract-authoring-receipt/v3`. Existing v1 evidence or v2 receipts are not silently reinterpreted.
- The raw-`unknown` Responses mapper preserves `input_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens` without requiring an SDK typing or dependency upgrade. Canonical mapping requires non-negative safe integers for input, cached read, cache write, output, reasoning, and total usage.
- Canonical usage fails closed when cache-write evidence is missing, negative, fractional, non-finite, unsafe, wrong-type, overflowing, or inconsistent. The enforced partition is `cachedInputTokens + cacheWriteInputTokens <= inputTokens`; reasoning cannot exceed output; total must equal input plus output without unsafe arithmetic.
- Attempt receipts and aggregate usage preserve `cacheWriteInputTokens`. Nominal cost prices cached reads at `$0.50/M`, cache writes at `$6.25/M`, remaining ordinary input at `$5/M`, and output at `$30/M`.
- The separate conservative calculation is unchanged: every input token remains reserved at the cache-write rate with the existing uplift. Maximum reservation remains exactly `$4.884`, the hard ceiling remains `$5.00`, application calls remain at most three, repairs remain at most two, and transport retries remain zero.
- Unbranded D1A0/injected adapters retain an explicit `legacy_injected_compatibility` path. Their missing cache-write count retains the historical zero compatibility default, but the durable attempt records that evidence kind. Missing or old/wrong brands cannot satisfy the canonical live requirement.
- Response ID, exact provider/model labels, completion status, sanitized response digest, write ordering, containment, candidate-only state, and all downstream separations are unchanged. Invalid evidence produces a failed sanitized receipt/readiness result and no candidate.
- No Story Source, cast, page, prop, location, reveal, or calibration literal was added to shared production code.

## R1D-PVB-D1A1A — Canonical Live Authoring Boundary

Guy approved the nine R1D-PVB-D1A1 architectural decisions and authorized D1A1A only. Commit `127335e7` implemented the initial zero-cost executable/provider-evidence boundary, `e069f19e` recorded its handoff, and Claude Code returned HOLD for that exact range. Corrective milestone `R1D-PVB-D1A1A-QA-FIX` remained inside the same zero-cost scope; code commit `e8a01182` fixed both MAJORs, all four MINORs, and the specifically authorized provider-storage/evidence-brand/prompt-authority advisory surfaces, with documentation through `925a3bf1`. Claude Code independently PASSed the exact correction and combined ranges recorded above. Guy then approved `R1D-PVB-D1A1A-RESIDUAL-HARDENING` to close seven formal residual MINOR findings without widening D1A1A. Code commit `2233e490` implements that correction. No D1A1A milestone performed live authoring or created real candidate, reconciliation, approval, Blueprint, Board, package, render, publication, production, or deployment authority.

### Independent QA PASS and residual hardening closeout

- **MAJOR — provider destination authority:** accepted. The installed SDK previously could consume `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, organization, project, or webhook environment defaults. The canonical child now filters the installed SDK's unauthorized OpenAI routing/identity/log names while preserving only future `OPENAI_API_KEY` credential authority. The adapter pins `https://api.openai.com/v1`, explicitly nulls organization/project/webhook settings, guards the exact HTTPS `POST /v1/responses` destination, forces redirect refusal, rejects identity headers, retains client/request retries `0`, and sends `store:false`.
- **MAJOR — spend before durable output authority:** accepted. The D1A1-scoped artifact store now resolves and realpath-containment-checks the output root and every category, rejects traversal/symlink escapes, creates and writable-probes all categories, and durably persists the exact source snapshot then approved live request before credential/provider reachability. After a handled call, receipt is the first write, followed by readiness and then candidate only on complete success.
- **MINOR — rejection reasons:** accepted. Rejected-request evidence now always contains non-empty, bounded, deduplicated, sorted stable reason codes, including `request_mode_must_be_live`, and persists no full request or raw untrusted/schema-owned value.
- **MINOR — sentinel arming:** accepted. The exact private-entry test hook now clears any stale marker, loads the credential/write sentinel, requires its same-path arming handshake, and exposes the directly checked armed result in canonical preflight output. Independent network/credential/write positive controls and fetch/sentinel restoration remain covered.
- **MINOR — canonical idempotence:** accepted. D1A1 evidence has a scoped human-readable stable serializer that sorts keys, NFC-normalizes strings and keys, and uses those same bytes for idempotence/collision comparison without changing historical lifecycle writers.
- **MINOR — page/budget boundary:** accepted. Under the current `$5` / three-call policy, up to 12 pages qualify. Thirteen or more fail before credential/provider reachability with `page_budget_partition_decision_required` and require a separate budget or partition Decision Gate.
- Additional hardening: canonical live requires exact `openai-responses-authoring-evidence/v1`; D1A0's injected-provider seam keeps an explicit optional requirement. The authoring request is now `visual-contract-authoring-request/v3`, with exact initial system/user prompt versions and digests plus repair system digest and compiler-owned repair user-builder version. The lifecycle validates actual prompt authority before every provider call. Live adapter/runner barrel exports were removed.
- Advisory limits retained: exact `gpt-5.6-sol` response-label equality remains fail-closed. A provider alias cannot be proven without a real call, so first-contact exact-label risk is documented rather than relaxed. The 64K input gate remains a conservative UTF-8 byte upper bound plus protocol allowance; no new tokenizer dependency or authority was introduced.

### Residual MINOR A-G disposition

- **A — logical/real repository split:** the canonical runner establishes one `repositoryRealPath` before reads and uses it for containment, output construction, and repository-relative artifact labels. A real junction/symlink-root test proves stable in-repository labels; unsupported link-fixture creation fails safely before provider construction.
- **B — camelCase reason loss:** stable rejected evidence admits only bounded alphanumeric/underscore/colon/hyphen codes while preserving schema-owned camelCase container labels. Exact durable assertions cover `structuredOutput`, `tokenBudget`, `callBudget`, `costBudget`, `promptDigests`, `promptAuthority`, `pricing`, and existing snake_case codes.
- **C — non-finite numbers:** the canonical digest and content-addressed byte writer now share the same canonicalization domain. Non-finite numbers fail before a request digest, raw-request persistence, or provider construction, with stable nullable-digest rejected evidence. Historical nested `undefined` JSON omission/null behavior remains byte-compatible for unrelated callers.
- **D — stale v2/missing prompt authority:** the request decoder is total and schema-owned. A stale v2 request without `promptAuthority` produces immutable sanitized evidence containing `request_version_mismatch` and `request_field_invalid:promptAuthority`; it cannot construct or reach a provider.
- **E — cross-writer bytes:** D1A0 source/request/receipt/readiness/candidate writers and D1A1 evidence use one canonical content-addressed JSON writer. A read-before-write migration accepts semantically identical historical pretty JSON without rewriting it, while truly different content at the same address retains the immutable collision failure.
- **F — failed sentinel arming:** a real canonical subprocess loads a deliberately non-arming sentinel whose filesystem read trap would fail if the CLI core import began. The private entry reports the arming failure first; the tripwire and preflight output remain unreachable. The positive arming assertion remains.
- **G — top-level scalar totality:** every required top-level request scalar is tested both omitted and wrong-type. Each case persists bounded stable rejection evidence and leaves provider construction unreachable rather than leaking a crypto or `TypeError`.
- Claude Code independently **PASSed** exact residual range `925a3bf1254f6c159125f7e8c34cc61f198d5847..1a93de55655676f86d4938e9e3888495e7555c7b` and combined D1A1A range `031bcb6aa08d4a9ee7cf527bb051970efc3e96d1..1a93de55655676f86d4938e9e3888495e7555c7b`, with no BLOCKER or MAJOR and all residual findings A-G independently verified closed.

### Canonical launcher and closed executable surface

- The sole public command is `node scripts/visual-contract-authoring.cjs`. It resolves the installed local `tsx/cli`, starts the child with the repository `server-only` preload before the guarded TypeScript entrypoint, and requires a one-time 64-hex capability carried independently in the child argument and environment.
- The launcher passes the existing process environment through without enumerating or printing credential-bearing values, but exposes a non-copying filtered child view whenever unauthorized installed-SDK routing/identity/log names exist. It temporarily adds only its capability key and restores that key even on child failure. The private entry consumes the capability before the CLI core loads. Direct private-entry and direct core subprocesses fail before CLI/provider work.
- The strict parser exposes only exclusive `preflight` and the future exact `live --repo-root ... --source-authority-request ... --snapshot ... --request ... --out ...` shape. Unknown/positional/duplicate/equals-form flags, missing values, mode mixtures, and help mixtures fail closed. Child errors, signals, integer statuses, and missing/nonstandard status propagate deterministically. Handled live failures persist evidence and return a nonzero child status.
- `preflight` accepts no Story Source, output, credential, model, price, or write authority. It imports the exact future adapter/runner graph under independent throwing raw-network plus credential/write sentinels. The private entry positively requires the credential/write sentinel arming marker before core import. `TSX_DISABLE_CACHE=1` isolates runtime compiler caching from the write boundary. Output reports only checked export/version/arming labels and explicitly says credentials, provider connectivity/configuration, prices, and billing were not checked.

### Exact Responses adapter and delayed credential boundary

- `openaiResponsesVisualContractAuthoringAdapter.ts` is the one D1A-only OpenAI Responses adapter. The generic provider pipeline is unchanged. D1A0's provider-unreachable commands and unrelated pipeline three-retry default remain compatible.
- Before credential reachability, the adapter independently enforces OpenAI / Responses / exact `gpt-5.6-sol` / service tier `default` / reasoning `medium` / strict `vc-draft-schema/v6` / tools disabled / provider storage disabled / no fallback / transport retries `0` / timeout `1,200,000 ms` / conservative input ceiling `64,000` / page-derived `32,000..64,000` output ceiling.
- Adapter construction performs no credential or provider work. Only `OPENAI_API_KEY` may be read from the existing process environment, inside `call`, after output containment/category/writable authority, durable source/request intent, and the lifecycle's exact source/snapshot/request/schema/price/spend/input/prompt/options gates pass. No env file or alternate credential source exists.
- The adapter maps branded sanitized Responses evidence: response ID, exact provider/model labels, completion status, input tokens, cached input tokens, output tokens, reasoning tokens, and total tokens. Every count must be a non-negative safe integer; cached/reasoning subtotals must fit; total must equal input plus output. Missing, malformed, inconsistent, overflowing, incomplete, failed, empty-output, missing-ID, or substituted evidence fails closed.
- Raw prompts, raw output, raw provider payload/exception/stack, credential/header/environment values, invalid drafts, and unknown request input fields are excluded from persisted request/receipt/readiness evidence. Existing unbranded injected-provider behavior remains available only when a non-canonical D1A0 caller explicitly omits the optional brand requirement; the public canonical live runner always requires the exact branded contract.

### Exact live authority, cost fence, and immutable evidence

### Exact source and request authority

- `visual-contract-authoring-request/v3` is the current shared request authority. It fixes OpenAI, Responses API, exact `gpt-5.6-sol`, standard/default tier, reasoning `medium`, strict `vc-draft-schema/v6`, tools disabled, `noFallback:true`, `transportRetries:0`, 20-minute timeout, 64,000 conservative input-token ceiling, page-derived output ceiling, initial call plus at most two repair calls, a `$5.00` hard ceiling, and exact initial/repair prompt authority. Any D1A0-era v2 artifact is historical pre-live evidence and cannot authorize the canonical live path.
- Price authority is versioned and digested from the official numeric table: uncached input `$5/M`, cache write `$6.25/M`, cached read `$0.50/M`, output `$30/M`, and regional uplift `1.10`. The preventive fence charges every possible input token at the cache-write rate and applies uplift.
- The 12-page calibration source derives a 36,000-token maximum output including reasoning, a 27,129 conservative prompt/schema input upper bound, and the exact three-call maximum `3 × ((64,000 × 6.25 + 36,000 × 30) / 1,000,000) × 1.10 = $4.884`. A general 13-page fixture projects `$5.181` and fails live preflight before its injected provider can be reached.
- Provider, model, endpoint, service tier, reasoning, strict schema, tools, fallback, retries, timeout, token, call, repair, pricing, pricing-digest, cost, prompt-digest, source-digest, and request-digest mismatches fail deterministic preflight. Re-digesting altered pricing does not restore authority. Before each possible call, conservative accounted spend plus every remaining per-call maximum reservation must remain at or below `$5`. Unrelated pipeline callers retain the existing three-transport-retry default; the exact authoring request performs zero transport retries.

### Structured action authority and separate review

- The strict authoring draft now requires a non-empty per-page `actionRequirements` array (`minItems:1`) from the existing closed predicate/polarity vocabulary plus exact same-page Story Source `sourcePhrase` evidence and an explicit `unsupportedActionSemantics` list. Evidence-only fields are removed from the contract before candidate assembly but retained as a separately digested, explicitly non-authoritative citation list in the immutable candidate review envelope.

Review bundle/Markdown versions are v2 because their content-addressed surface exposes that binding. Reconciliation remains a separate pending artifact that also binds the exact Visual Contract digest and grants no Blueprint, Board, package, render, publication, release, or deployment authority.

This record does not self-award PASS; Claude Code must micro re-gate the combined original and corrective ranges.

### Required recurring-props boundary and no-throw defense

- `BookVisualContractTemplate` validation now explicitly rejects omitted or non-array `recurringProps`. A legitimate story with no recurring props must author the required empty array; malformed durable input is no longer made indistinguishable from that valid shape.
- PVB independently requires an indexable recurring-prop array before building its contract index, so an upstream validator regression cannot expose the original raw `.map` path. Contract indexing, cover lifecycle projection, page lifecycle helpers, and the final reveal scan all use guarded durable-array reads as defense in depth.
- The malformed-input matrix now includes omitted, `null`, object, string, and malformed-array recurring-prop forms. Each is deterministically restamped where normalization permits, returns identical structured `ok:false` issues across repeated calls, never throws, and causes the assertion API to emit only `InvalidPreRenderBookVisualBlueprintError`.
- The canonical validator regressions prove deterministic rejection of the four missing/non-array forms and safe rejection of a malformed array entry. All existing Template fixtures already carried the required array, so no compatibility fixture migration or permissive legacy fallback was needed.

### Canonical transition-kind semantics

- The connection graph now requires each traversal authority to use either authored endpoint and a direction permitted by the connection. Endpoint placement no longer incorrectly encodes arrival state.
- Frame validation remains pinned to the existing exact page/frame zone authority: `before_transition` uses the origin, `after_transition` uses the destination, and `threshold` may use either authored endpoint. Visible traversal and opening-clearance support must be in that exact frame zone; numerically overlapping geometry from the opposite endpoint cannot qualify.
- Direction is still checked against the authored connection orientation. Tests cover valid origin `before_transition`, invalid destination `before_transition`, valid destination `after_transition`, both valid threshold endpoints, opposite-endpoint support with identical geometry, forward and reverse bidirectional traversal, incompatible direction, and a multi-element traversal-authority list.
- The canonical transition state machine was not changed. The reordered neutral-geometry reveal regression now also proves it is not passing through unrelated `template_stale` evidence.

The six failures are the unchanged absent ignored-output baseline in `child-lexicon-ages-5-8.spec.ts`, `momentum-gate-koko.spec.ts`, `page-entity-qa.spec.ts`, `set-appearance-ref-budget.spec.ts`, and two `story-read-back-validation.spec.ts` cases. ## R1D-PVB-A-QA-FIX — corrective implementation awaiting independent re-gate

Claude Code returned **HOLD** on immutable original range `0734efc21549ab5d449bdb5960b398c0f5196d3c..1552741b5716862d0ebca2e2daf071d15714472e`. The Lead independently accepted all three MAJOR and two MINOR findings: malformed durable input could leak raw `TypeError`; reveal-name matching discarded short semantic tokens and required contiguous/in-order text; declared support/action capacities and traversal clearance were not enforced; transition affordances were not pinned to the authored destination zone/direction; and digest-owned ordering used locale-sensitive `localeCompare`. Corrective code commit `f805a6b1a4f4da9088cea23a0c7b0f46330e2471` addresses those accepted findings. This record does not self-award PASS; Claude Code must re-gate the combined original-plus-correction range.

### Fail-closed durable-input contract

- Validator helpers now operate on validated local arrays/records rather than rescanning malformed raw placements, cast arrays, consumers, supported entities, continuity arrays, connection arrays, or reveal placements. Missing nested source/template/package identities receive structured schema/staleness issues without hashing `undefined`.
- Canonical equality has a deterministic serialization representation for missing values. Digest normalization failure still records `schema_invalid` and cannot make malformed content acceptable; there is no broad validator catch hiding downstream defects.
- A table-driven malformed-input matrix covers missing source/template/package identity, null placement/subject/cast/camera/lifecycle, non-array frame affordances, null affordance consumers and support entities, null safety targets, non-array carryover/transition arrays, null connection endpoints, and null supporting geometry. Every case proves repeated validation returns identical `ok:false` issues, never throws, and `assertValidPreRenderBookVisualBlueprint` throws only `InvalidPreRenderBookVisualBlueprintError`.
- The typed validation context remains a caller-trusted boundary and was not widened to `unknown`; no runtime caller or context contract changed.

### Reveal semantics, bounded feasibility, and transition direction

- Reveal detection now uses deterministic NFKC Unicode tokenization. Latin/Hebrew letter/number runs and individual Han/Hiragana/Katakana/Hangul glyphs are exact tokens; no minimum token length remains. Multi-token names use exact token-multiset containment, so separated or reordered names are caught without substring matching.
- Generic identifier namespaces are removed only from the beginning of prop IDs and only while another semantic token remains. Tests cover `TV`, `Ax`, a generic-looking `Object`, one-glyph Hebrew and CJK names, reordered `red bucket`, substring controls such as `tax`/`axle`, and incomplete multi-token controls. The same matcher governs frame narrative and spoiler-neutral geometry descriptions.
- Required prop and action requirements must resolve to exactly one compatible, consumer-declared frame affordance. Unique required prop IDs are aggregated per placement support and checked against `maximumOccupants`; unique required-action `actorId` cast members are aggregated per action space and checked against `maximumActors`. An acted-upon cast object is not counted as a second actor. Exact-capacity and joint-overbooking tests cover both bounds.
- Traversal clearance is now the deterministic `min(width, height)` of normalized-1000 geometry. Every connection-linked traversal footprint and same-zone opening `clearanceRegion` must meet its integer `minimumClearance`; exact-boundary, narrow-traversal, and narrow-opening cases are covered.
- The still-unapproved blueprint v1 connection field evolved from singular `traversalAffordanceId` to explicit `traversalAffordanceIds`. Each traversal direction is pinned to its destination endpoint zone; frame validation selects a destination-zone, direction-compatible affordance. Bidirectional reverse transitions remain explicitly representable, while wrong-direction and unrelated-zone support fail closed. No external or runtime consumer of this held artifact exists.
- Digest-owned arrays and deterministic issue ordering now use an explicit locale-independent UTF-16 lexical comparator. A non-ASCII ordering regression proves stable ordering without `localeCompare`.

## R1D-PVB-A — original Pre-Render Book Visual Blueprint implementation (Claude Code HOLD)

Guy approved the zero-cost `R1D-PVB-A — Schema and Feasibility` milestone under the `R1D-PRE-RENDER-BOOK-VISUAL-BLUEPRINT` Decision Gate. Repository investigation confirmed that the existing Visual Contract / Visual Package already owns structured world, cast, prop lifecycle, page action, safety, source identity, and package evidence, but final composition authority is still created later by a page-only runtime storyboard LLM plus a separate per-page director/fallback path. This milestone adds the missing book-level structured composition and feasibility authority without cutting over either runtime path. It makes no independent technical PASS claim; Claude Code first-pass read-only review is still required.

### Versioned authority and deterministic binding

- New additive artifact `pre-render-book-visual-blueprint/v1` lives inside `lib/visual-package` as an explicit successor authority, not a parallel top-level subsystem. It embeds the exact validated `BookVisualContractTemplate` once and binds the exact normalized Story Source identity/digest, template identity/digest, and candidate Visual Package artifact identity/content digest.
- The artifact has a canonical JSON SHA-256 digest. Pure finalization clones and deterministically sorts order-insensitive arrays before hashing, does not mutate caller-owned drafts, and produces stable serialization across object-key and input-array ordering. Any schema, content, or stored-digest mutation fails closed.
- The whole-story world plan adds only the composition data absent from the current Visual Contract: typed authored connections, traversal/opening clearance, placement support, action footprint, camera access, safe-boundary affordances, and explicitly spoiler-neutral supporting geometry. Stable IDs and discriminated consumers link frames, actions, placements, transitions, and safety constraints back to the embedded contract.
- One structured frame is required for the cover and every exact Story Source page. Each frame has the invariant numeric aspect ratio `{ width: 2, height: 3 }`, normalized integer coordinates in `0..1000`, narrative purpose/beat, exact contract-projected location/zone/cast/prop lifecycle, deterministic blocking/depth regions, camera authority, text-safe region, continuity/carryover, and referenced affordances.

### Fail-closed feasibility and reveal validation

- Validation requires one cover plus exact page-number coverage with no missing, duplicate, or extra frame and rejects unresolved or duplicate location, zone, connection, cast, prop, action, affordance, geometry, frame, and consumer references.
- Frame location, zone, cast, prop lifecycle, transition kind, and previous-frame chain must be exact deterministic projections of the embedded Visual Contract. Current Story Source, template, and candidate Visual Package context are recomputed and compared, so any authoritative source/package change makes the blueprint stale.
- Every required cast member, required prop, and must-action needs exactly one key bounded placement. Required prop placement must fit a compatible typed support/anchor affordance; required action evidence must fit an action-space affordance with compatible predicate and entities; camera visibility must contain all key evidence.
- Text-safe collision is deterministic rectangle intersection, not subjective prose. The declared text-safe region may not overlap any key cast, must-action, or required-prop evidence.
- Non-steady page transitions must follow an authored zone connection and carry visible traversal support, any declared opening-clearance support, and the page's applicable safety boundaries. Required props may not block opening clearance. Safety constraints require a compatible safe boundary that contains the subject placement.
- Before `firstRevealPage`, hidden narrative props are forbidden in placements, carryover, and narrative naming. Supporting geometry is allowed only when explicitly `spoilerNeutral: true`, structurally bound to declared stable geometry, and neither bound to nor described with the future prop identity. This permits stable support/path/space planning without depicting the reveal object.

### Generalization, compatibility, and validation evidence

- Compact synthetic fixtures cover five different story shapes through the same shared code: single-location, multi-zone transition, journey/fantastical, no-companion, and reveal-timeline. Shared schema/validator modules contain no Fox, Uri, bucket, bedroom, balcony, or fixed-page literal.
- The new focused suite passes **25/25 tests**, including all five shapes; exact 2:3 cover/page authority; coverage/reference failures; deterministic serialization and non-mutating finalization; schema/content/digest tampering; malformed-input issue returns; action, placement, traversal, opening, camera, safety, and text-safe infeasibility; reveal-safe geometry and spoiler violations; Story Source/template/package staleness; and the typed assertion API.
- Relevant regression validation passes **7 files / 154 tests** across the new blueprint suite, Visual Package lifecycle, source/prompt reconciliation, spoiler-safe prop authority, and Visual Contract template/stage 3/stage 4.
- `npx --no-install tsc --noEmit`, `git diff --check`, and cached diff checks: **PASS**.
- Literal `npm run check`: TypeScript **PASS**; Vitest **254 files total — 233 passed, 16 skipped, 5 failed; 2,543 tests total — 2,472 passed, 65 skipped, 6 failed**. The six failures are exactly the established absent ignored-output fixture baseline in `child-lexicon-ages-5-8.spec.ts`, `momentum-gate-koko.spec.ts`, `page-entity-qa.spec.ts`, `set-appearance-ref-budget.spec.ts`, and two cases in `story-read-back-validation.spec.ts`. No new or changed test failed. No missing ignored fixture was copied, fabricated, or imported. The four ordinary ignored scratch files created by the full run were removed afterward.
- Compatibility is additive: `visual-package/v3` candidate/review/approval/promotion qualification and existing runtime storyboard/director/fallback behavior are unchanged. Existing packages do not acquire blueprint authority or become render-qualified, and there is no schema/data migration.

### Boundaries, limitations, and next gates

- No live authoring/model/LLM call, image/page/full-book render, provider/OpenAI/Vision/fetch/network/credential use, storage/Supabase/database read or write, Set Identity Board action, candidate/approval/promotion/deployment, production flag, threshold change, merge, rebase, push, or worktree/branch cleanup occurred. Tests use only synthetic in-memory data and existing local mocks.
- PVB-A provides schema, pure finalization/digest, validators, exports, fixtures, and tests only. It does not generate a production blueprint for any current story, persist one, expose a review UI, or define approval lifecycle. PVB-B must separately design and approve authoring/review/persistence/lifecycle integration.
- Existing runtime page storyboard, director, prompt assembly, and fallback paths remain active and independently mutable until a separately approved PVB-C cutover. PVB-C must make downstream prose/prompts/boards deterministic projections of reviewed blueprint authority and remove or gate the legacy independent truths; this milestone does not imply that cutover.

## R1D GPT Image request-options hardening - independent technical QA PASS

Guy explicitly approved `R1D-GPT-IMAGE-REQUEST-OPTIONS-HARDENING` as a general zero-cost corrective milestone after canonical board-mint attempt 2 failed in local OpenAI SDK request validation. The code correction is focused commit `cec084943ba2799b50ef4a35e1db5e5dfe89956f`, with implementation record at `d39b6eb80e49d1841e8873465829573986abc167`. Claude Code independently reviewed the exact immutable range `294e6ba1952036c3745ddeada4c23ac669929b0a..d39b6eb80e49d1841e8873465829573986abc167` and returned **PASS**, with no BLOCKER, MAJOR, or MINOR and six non-blocking NOTEs. This PASS is attributed to Claude Code and is scoped only to that immutable fix range; it is not Board, product, visual, promotion, deployment, or release acceptance and does not authorize a live mint rerun.

### Verified root cause and general correction

- `planGPTImageRequest` previously created own `signal` and `timeout` properties even when their values were `undefined`. The installed OpenAI SDK is `6.35.0`; its `buildRequest` checks `'timeout' in options`, validates that value with `validatePositiveInteger`, and only then applies the SDK default. A caller that omitted `requestTimeoutMs` therefore reached `images.generate` or `images.edit` with an own `timeout: undefined` property and failed locally with `timeout must be an integer`.
- The defect was in the shared GPT Image request planner, not in Fox, Set Board, or the mint launcher. Repository call-site review found multiple shared generation paths that intentionally omit `requestTimeoutMs`, while page-generation paths that own a bounded deadline pass an explicit positive timeout. Both `images.generate` and `images.edit` consume the same planned request options.
- The planner now starts from mandatory `{ maxRetries: 0 }` and conditionally adds `signal` and `timeout` only when the caller supplied them. This preserves the existing no-hidden-SDK-retry policy, explicit AbortSignal forwarding, and explicit timeout forwarding without inventing a Board-specific timeout or changing model, quality, retry, reference, QA, or storage policy.
- An explicitly supplied `requestTimeoutMs` must now be a finite positive integer. Zero, negative, fractional, `NaN`, and either infinity fail locally with a clear planner error before either OpenAI image method can be invoked. Existing TypeScript callers already use millisecond numbers; no schema or data migration is required.

### Zero-network compatibility and regression evidence

- Focused request-option validation: **PASS - 2 files / 19 tests**. Coverage distinguishes property absence from an own property whose value is `undefined`; covers no optional options, signal-only, valid timeout-only, invalid timeouts, both generate/edit paths, no SDK image-method invocation for invalid values, explicit timeout forwarding, and real AbortSignal handler propagation.
- Relevant shared-call-path validation: **PASS - 6 files / 87 tests** across GPT Image request planning/cancellation, the installed-SDK compatibility test, set-appearance board model selection, board-mint launcher, runtime world authority, and Style 01 QA regeneration.
- The compatibility test imports the actual installed OpenAI SDK `6.35.0`, supplies the explicit fake key `test-key-never-sent`, blanks the process API-key environment for the test, uses `https://network.invalid/v1`, and injects the same throwing fetch sentinel both into the client and as global fetch. With timeout and signal absent, SDK option validation accepted `{ maxRetries: 0 }`, reached the injected fetch boundary exactly once, and surfaced the sentinel error. No real key or credential file was loaded, and the client had no network-capable fetch path.
- `npx --no-install tsc --noEmit` and `git diff --check`: **PASS**.
- Proportional full validation with `npm run check -- --silent --maxWorkers=4 --reporter=dot`: TypeScript **PASS**; Vitest **253 files total - 232 passed, 16 skipped, 5 failed; 2,518 tests total - 2,447 passed, 65 skipped, 6 failed**. The six failures are exactly the established absent ignored-output fixture baseline in `child-lexicon-ages-5-8.spec.ts`, `momentum-gate-koko.spec.ts`, `page-entity-qa.spec.ts`, `set-appearance-ref-budget.spec.ts`, and two cases in `story-read-back-validation.spec.ts`. No changed or new request-option test failed under full-suite load.
- The full suite created the four ordinary ignored synthetic files under `outputs/qa-anchors/test_lion__fp__wardrobe` and `outputs/test-fixtures/story-read-back-regression`; only those exact files were removed after each run. The historical ignored Fox dry preview was preserved, with observed SHA-256 `f1b91a39d6ade95905b7d84fcf135b0158cfc8f477ad4d5ebac3b4ace04c9b0b`.

### Independent-QA note dispositions

- The soft-timeout layer's `<= 0` disabled sentinel does not match `requestTimeoutMs`, which now rejects every non-positive explicit value. Claude Code identified this as a real future-contract risk, but all current production timeout producers resolve to at least `30,000ms`, so the mismatch is presently unreachable. Track it for a separate general timeout-policy follow-up; do not patch it in this closed gate.
- The real installed-SDK compatibility test crosses `images.generate` only. `images.edit` uses the same SDK `buildRequest` option-validation path, and the repository's edit mocks cover forwarding, omission, cancellation, and invalid-timeout callback unreachability. Direct installed-SDK edit coverage remains a non-blocking coverage note.
- Callers without an explicit deadline now correctly receive the OpenAI SDK default timeout of `600,000ms`; this is the intended restored behavior after removing `timeout: undefined`. Alignment between that default and worker execution budgets should be evaluated in a separate general timeout-policy milestone before broad production rollout, not through a Board-specific timeout.
- TypeScript `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is disabled in this repository, so compile-time checking alone cannot distinguish an omitted optional property from an own property set to `undefined`. The `hasOwnProperty` regression assertions are therefore intentional and must be preserved.
- Conditional omission of an absent `signal` is defensive consistency. The observed canonical Board failure was caused by the SDK's treatment of the `timeout` property, not by `signal`.
- The legacy raw-fetch DALL-E 3 path bypasses `planGPTImageRequest` and its cancellation/request-option policy. It is outside the current `gpt-image-2` Board path and this immutable review range; track a separate legacy-path audit before production release.

### Boundaries, limitations, rollback, and next action

- No image render, OpenAI image or Vision provider request, real network request, reference download, Supabase/storage/database operation, registry/candidate write, approval, promotion, deployment, push, flag change, threshold change, or production action occurred. The compatibility test's zero-network claim is supported by its explicit client-level and global throwing fetch sentinel plus the one observed sentinel call. Other exercised provider/storage seams were local mocks. No independent OpenAI provider-account usage/billing audit or Supabase provider-log audit was performed.
- The correction does not alter the previously failed attempt evidence, create a Fox v3 board, or provide visual/product acceptance. There remains no current v3 board for Guy to eyeball.
- Rollback is a focused revert of the code and state-record commits; there is no migration.
- The independent technical review gate is closed with Claude Code PASS for the exact immutable range above. A future paid attempt still requires a new explicit Guy live-rerun authorization; this documentation closeout does not supply it.

- CLI parsing now rejects unknown flags, positional arguments, duplicate flags, every `--flag=value` spelling, incompatible mode flags, and help combined with any other argument. `--preflight-live-imports=1` therefore fails closed rather than becoming a mint. - The subprocess sentinel patches raw TCP before TLS/HTTP/HTTPS load and distinguishes default-loopback `net.connect(port)` from explicit remote-host signatures, including numeric-string ports, option objects, `net.createConnection`, `net.Socket.prototype.connect`, and HTTP/HTTPS Agent connection paths. It explicitly says that credentials, provider connectivity, and provider-side configuration were not validated. It reports the actually observed fetch-attempt count and does not print invented render/upload/Vision/registry/approval counters. ### General stable authority and fail-closed boundaries

- `BookVisualContract.setBoardAuthorities` is an optional reviewed, structured, board-only source layer. Each required board binds stable locations, board areas/nodes/relations, recurring fixed objects, and placements without importing page-rich location lighting, spatial descriptions, zones, story action, or reveal prose. Stories with no requested board remain backward-compatible; pending/ready required boards fail closed when their stable authority is missing, malformed, incomplete, or inconsistent with the reviewed contract.
- Stable-authority validation requires exact location coverage, one stable board area per location, closed node/relation vocabulary, valid recurring fixed objects and placements, and excludes lifecycle-gated or page-forbidden props. Compiler, repair, materialization, live-authoring, template validation, and runtime-world identity propagation carry the new structured authority.
- Set definition, registry, content-policy, and prompt-guard semantics are explicitly versioned as `set-board/v3`, `set-registry/v3`, `set-board-content/v2`, and `set-board-positive-authority/v1`. Prior v1/v2 board and registry evidence cannot confer current authority. `materializer/v1` is intentionally unchanged so no-board and already-materialized non-board flows remain compatible; the optional stable authority participates in the runtime-world projection digest when present.
- Every active style and alias now resolves through an explicitly authored board-safe `setBoard` style block. Board style is projected from those structured fields, never by stripping phrases from page-oriented prompt prose.
- Positive-authority guards deterministically reject generic cast language, exact cast IDs/labels, actions, transient/story/sound/reveal language, portable-light authority, and every recurring prop outside `contentPolicy.includedPropIds`. Blocked cast and prop terms come from structured contract identities rather than runtime semantic inference or current-story vocabulary. Negative prompts and QA forbids remain allowed to name blocked entities and actions.
- The direct definition/prompt path requires the current guard policy and rechecks all positive sources. The real board-mint orchestration test proves a blocked transient prop fails before render, upload, or QA callbacks are reachable. Shared implementation contains no Fox, Uri, flashlight, bucket, ticking, rhythm, or page-number literal.

## R1D Board Free-Text Spoiler Guard - unified independent QA PASS

Claude Code independently reviewed the completed R1D Board Spoiler-Safe Authority range `f5a248a0887cfc6e841a4a5040d2447eafee9fcc..3b52cf2333dc4e85cc9e034f9dc254f753623523` and returned **PASS**, with one valid MINOR hardening finding. Guy authorized this separate zero-cost corrective milestone on the same sole-writer branch/worktree. The correction and its stability-test follow-up are committed locally and unpushed. Claude Code's later unified review through `b65f5bbb411c17cb3666493a67d0993d5ca9cdf0` independently verified the correction, callback unreachability, negative-authority allowance, generality, and test-only stability repair and returned **PASS** with no remaining defect.

### Validated root risk and general correction

- `set-board/v2` correctly excluded structured reveal-gated prop nodes, facts, and relations, but positive free text still reached `buildSetIdentityBoardPrompt`. A future location name, lighting string, retained zone geometry description, fixed-fact material/scale, set identity/facet, or registered positive style block could therefore reintroduce an excluded prop before board mint even though the structured projection and negative policy were correct.
- A new pure, deterministic `assertSetBoardPositiveAuthorityIsSpoilerNeutral` invariant rejects that leak with typed `SetBoardPositiveAuthoritySpoilerError` code `set_board_positive_authority_spoiler_leak`. The error identifies the set, exact field path/provenance, excluded structured prop ID/name, and matched canonical term. There is no silent scrubbing, mutation, warning-only behavior, I/O, network, clock, or randomness.
- Canonical terms are explainably derived from structured prop identity/name: normalized full name, semantic ID after leading namespace tokens such as `prop`, `props`, `recurring`, `item`, or `object`, and a semantic head shared by the ID/name when available. Unicode NFKC, lowercase word tokens, and exact token-sequence boundaries catch case/punctuation variants and the approved partial-name example while containing short-token, substring, and common-prefix false positives. There is no fuzzy matching, stemming, story vocabulary, page-number rule, or Fox-specific branch.
- The central projection invariant checks every positive source actually emitted by the prompt: set identity and board version/facet; location name, time, lighting, and environment class; retained zone geometry; fixed-fact name, material, and scale; and registered positive style prompt. A defense-in-depth check also protects callers that construct a `SetBoardDefinition` and invoke the prompt builder directly. Non-prose local aliases and the closed relation/opening vocabulary are documented at the invariant.
- Intentional generated `NO <excluded prop>` content-policy and negative-prompt lines are outside the positive-authority scan and remain present. Useful legitimate set, lighting, geometry, material, and style authority is preserved.
- The invariant runs in the shared set-definition/prompt path used before mint can invoke its render callback. Caller-level mint coverage proves a leaking definition fails before render, upload, or QA callbacks are reachable. Existing Fox v2 projection/prompt remains valid and spoiler-neutral; historical v1 board bytes and registry evidence are untouched.
- Shared guard/projection/prompt code contains no Fox story key, bucket name, or page-5 literal.

### Focused coverage

- New adversarial unit coverage exercises location-name and exact `FLASHLIGHT on the bucket!` lighting leaks, retained geometry, fixed material, set identity/facet, positive style, direct prompt-builder construction, allowed negative policy, punctuation/case, short names, substring containment, common `prop_` prefixes, and clean general/Fox projections.
- Caller-level mint coverage proves callback unreachability for a positive-authority leak. Existing projection, prompt, board QA, mint, runtime-reference, lifecycle, compatibility, and Fox tests remain green.
- Final relevant regression run: **PASS - 14 files / 282 tests**. Isolated corrected budget contract: **PASS - 1 file / 3 tests**.
